{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Y42QNpiAa3ovrQPaExioRf","type":"tool_result","content":"# Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n----\n# Definition of Done\n\n> #143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)` inside the server process. The client on the other end of the WebSocket has no way to ask for it, and the consumer who wants a conversation picker without standing up their own REST endpoint has to write one anyway.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `createServer` and the WebSocket upgrade/message pipeline already exist and accept frames over a real WebSocket connection, so acceptance tests can connect today, send `list_sessions` frames, and fail directly on the missing `session_list` and `list_sessions` validation/dispatch behaviour.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId** — Sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit, before, userId}` over a connected WebSocket and verifies `store.listFor` is called with the socket's authenticated `userId` (`session.userId`) and `{limit, before}`, answering `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws** — Verifies that a store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}` and a store whose `listFor` throws/rejects logs an error under category `'ws'` with the userId and error message and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor** — Verifies that `{type: 'list_sessions'}` is accepted while non-integer or negative `limit` values and malformed `before` cursors (`sessionId` outside 1–128 chars or unparseable `updatedAt`) are rejected with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without invoking `store.listFor`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Add `ListSessionsMessage`, `SessionSummaryFrame`, and `SessionListMessage` to `src/types/messages.ts`, include them in `IncomingMessage` and `OutgoingMessage`, and re-export them from `src/index.ts`.\n- [ ] Validate `list_sessions` frames in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (optional non-negative integer `limit`; optional `before` object with 1–128 char `sessionId` and finite-date `updatedAt` string or number; strip unknown fields like `userId`).\n  - [x] Accept a bare `{type: 'list_sessions'}` frame (`ListSessionsMessage` added to `IncomingMessage`).\n  - [x] Reject malformed `limit` with `INVALID_MESSAGE` (non-integer or negative); `userId` is already stripped by the returned frame.\n  - [x] Reject a `before` that is not an object with `INVALID_MESSAGE`.\n  - [x] Reject a `before` whose `sessionId` is not 1–128 chars with `INVALID_MESSAGE`.\n  - [ ] Reject a `before` whose `updatedAt` is not parseable with `INVALID_MESSAGE`.\n  - [ ] Forward `limit`/`before` to the handler.\n- [ ] Thread `sessionStore` into `createMessageHandler` (optional parameter so existing 1-arg and 2-arg callers keep working) and wire it in `src/transport/createServer.ts`.\n- [ ] Handle `list_sessions` in `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.ts` (and helper in `src/transport/ws/sender.ts` if needed) by reading `session.userId`, calling `await sessionStore?.listFor?.(session.userId, {limit, before})`, serialising `Date` fields to ISO strings, and on error logging via `log.error('ws', ...)` and sending `ErrorCodes.SERVER_ERROR`.\n- [ ] Document `list_sessions` and `session_list` in `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` and `specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md`.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Conversation titles or previews (first user message, model-generated title).\n- Deleting or renaming a conversation from the client over the socket.\n- Switching the socket's active session in place (`?sessionId=` on reconnect is how a conversation is rejoined).\n- Holding pagination cursor state server-side (the client pages with `before`).\n- An operator view of another user's conversations or live sync between open tabs.\n----\n## CI reported failures on cc1db0ca122d32b88c3af9c59c8feaf88edc3364\n\nThe build for your last push is red. Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n----\n# Definition of Done\n\n> #143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)` inside the server process. The client on the other end of the WebSocket has no way to ask for it, and the consumer who wants a conversation picker without standing up their own REST endpoint has to write one anyway.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `createServer` and the WebSocket upgrade/message pipeline already exist and accept frames over a real WebSocket connection, so acceptance tests can connect today, send `list_sessions` frames, and fail directly on the missing `session_list` and `list_sessions` validation/dispatch behaviour.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId** — Sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit, before, userId}` over a connected WebSocket and verifies `store.listFor` is called with the socket's authenticated `userId` (`session.userId`) and `{limit, before}`, answering `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws** — Verifies that a store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}` and a store whose `listFor` throws/rejects logs an error under category `'ws'` with the userId and error message and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor** — Verifies that `{type: 'list_sessions'}` is accepted while non-integer or negative `limit` values and malformed `before` cursors (`sessionId` outside 1–128 chars or unparseable `updatedAt`) are rejected with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without invoking `store.listFor`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Add `ListSessionsMessage`, `SessionSummaryFrame`, and `SessionListMessage` to `src/types/messages.ts`, include them in `IncomingMessage` and `OutgoingMessage`, and re-export them from `src/index.ts`.\n- [ ] Validate `list_sessions` frames in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (optional non-negative integer `limit`; optional `before` object with 1–128 char `sessionId` and finite-date `updatedAt` string or number; strip unknown fields like `userId`).\n  - [x] Accept a bare `{type: 'list_sessions'}` frame (`ListSessionsMessage` added to `IncomingMessage`).\n  - [x] Reject malformed `limit` with `INVALID_MESSAGE` (non-integer or negative); `userId` is already stripped by the returned frame.\n  - [x] Reject a `before` that is not an object with `INVALID_MESSAGE`.\n  - [x] Reject a `before` whose `sessionId` is not 1–128 chars with `INVALID_MESSAGE`.\n  - [ ] Reject a `before` whose `updatedAt` is not parseable with `INVALID_MESSAGE`.\n  - [ ] Forward `limit`/`before` to the handler.\n- [ ] Thread `sessionStore` into `createMessageHandler` (optional parameter so existing 1-arg and 2-arg callers keep working) and wire it in `src/transport/createServer.ts`.\n- [ ] Handle `list_sessions` in `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.ts` (and helper in `src/transport/ws/sender.ts` if needed) by reading `session.userId`, calling `await sessionStore?.listFor?.(session.userId, {limit, before})`, serialising `Date` fields to ISO strings, and on error logging via `log.error('ws', ...)` and sending `ErrorCodes.SERVER_ERROR`.\n- [ ] Document `list_sessions` and `session_list` in `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` and `specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md`.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Conversation titles or previews (first user message, model-generated title).\n- Deleting or renaming a conversation from the client over the socket.\n- Switching the socket's active session in place (`?sessionId=` on reconnect is how a conversation is rejoined).\n- Holding pagination cursor state server-side (the client pages with `before`).\n- An operator view of another user's conversations or live sync between open tabs.\n----\n## CI reported failures on {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SjgPrhQnuYQHiRJpGKrcf5","type":"tool_result","content":"import type {IncomingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\n\nconst MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH = 10000;\nconst MAX_SESSION_ID_LENGTH = 128;\n\ninterface ValidationSuccess {\n  valid: true;\n  data: IncomingMessage;\n}\n\ninterface ValidationFailure {\n  valid: false;\n  error: string;\n}\n\nexport type ValidationResult = ValidationSuccess | ValidationFailure;\n\nexport function validateMessage(payload: unknown): ValidationResult {\n  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must be an object'};\n  }\n\n  const message = payload as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n  if (typeof message.type !== 'string') {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must have a type field'};\n  }\n\n  // eslint-disable-next-line re-lint/prefer-polymorphism -- a discriminated union narrows the untrusted frame; see adrs/ADR-006-lint-suppressions.md\n  switch (message.type) {\n    case 'user_message':\n      return validateUserMessage(message);\n    case 'ping':\n      return validatePingMessage(message);\n    case 'list_sessions':\n      return validateListSessionsMessage(message);\n    default:\n      return {valid: false, error: `Unknown message type: ${message.type}`};\n  }\n}\n\nfunction validateUserMessage(message: Record<string, unknown>): ValidationResult {\n  if (typeof message.content !== 'string') {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'user_message must have content string'};\n  }\n\n  const trimmed = message.content.trim();\n\n  if (trimmed.length === 0) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message content cannot be empty'};\n  }\n\n  if (message.content.length > MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH) {\n    return {valid: false, error: `Message content too long (max ${MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH} chars)`};\n  }\n\n  return {\n    valid: true,\n    data: {\n      type: 'user_message',\n      content: message.content,\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction validateListSessionsMessage(message: Record<string, unknown>): ValidationResult {\n  const {limit, before} = message;\n\n  if (limit !== undefined && !isNonNegativeInteger(limit)) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'list_sessions limit must be a non-negative integer'};\n  }\n\n  if (before !== undefined && !isCursorObject(before)) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'list_sessions before must be a cursor object'};\n  }\n\n  if (before !== undefined && !isValidSessionId(before.sessionId)) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'list_sessions before sessionId must be 1-128 characters'};\n  }\n\n  return {valid: true, data: {type: 'list_sessions'}};\n}\n\nfunction isNonNegativeInteger(value: unknown): boolean {\n  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isInteger(value) && value >= 0;\n}\n\nfunction isCursorObject(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {\n  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null;\n}\n\nfunction isValidSessionId(sessionId: unknown): boolean {\n  return typeof sessionId === 'string' && sessionId.length >= 1 && sessionId.length <= MAX_SESSION_ID_LENGTH;\n}\n\nfunction validatePingMessage(message: Record<string, unknown>): ValidationResult {\n  if (typeof message.timestamp !== 'number') {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'ping must have timestamp number'};\n  }\n\n  return {\n    valid: true,\n    data: {\n      type: 'ping',\n      timestamp: message.timestamp,\n    },\n  };\n}\n----\nimport {validateMessage} from './validation.js';\n\n// One wire name for the client frame: the alias is gone, and the frame the validator returns is the exported shape.\n\ndescribe('the websocket frame validator', () => {\n  it('accepts user_message and returns the frame the exported union describes', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: true, data: {type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'}});\n  });\n\n  it('rejects send_message, which is no longer a wire name', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'send_message', content: 'hello'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: false, error: 'Unknown message type: send_message'});\n  });\n\n  it('accepts a bare list_sessions frame, so the list needs no client userId', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'list_sessions'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: true, data: {type: 'list_sessions'}});\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a list_sessions limit that is not a non-negative integer', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'list_sessions', limit: 1.5});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: false, error: 'list_sessions limit must be a non-negative integer'});\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a list_sessions before cursor that is not an object', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'list_sessions', before: 'not-an-object'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: false, error: 'list_sessions before must be a cursor object'});\n  });\n\n  it('drops any field the frame does not define, rather than forwarding it', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({type: 'user_message', content: 'hello', chatId: 'c1'});\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: true, data: {type: 'user_message', content: 'hello'}});\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a list_sessions before cursor whose sessionId is not 1-128 characters', () => {\n    const result = validateMessage({\n      type: 'list_sessions',\n      before: {sessionId: '', updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z'},\n    });\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({valid: false, error: 'list_sessions before sessionId must be 1-128 characters'});\n  });\n});\n----\nimport type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {Express} from 'express';\nimport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\nimport {answeringOrchestrator, delegatingTo, eventually} from './wsTestSupport.js';\nimport {InMemorySessionStore} from '../infrastructure/stores/inMemorySessionStore.js';\nimport type {SessionCursor, SessionListOptions, SessionStore, SessionSummary} from '../types/sessionStore.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../shared/logger.js';\n\n// Real upgrades over a real socket: what the list_sessions frame answers is the contract, and no double can observe it.\n\ninterface Frame {\n  type: string;\n  sessionId?: string;\n  sessions?: Array<{sessionId: string; createdAt: string; updatedAt: string; entryCount: number}>;\n  code?: string;\n  message?: string;\n}\n\nconst servers: Array<ReturnType<typeof createServer>> = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\nconst logLines: Array<{level: string; category: string; message: string; fields?: Record<string, unknown>}> = [];\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  logLines.length = 0;\n  const record = (level: string) => (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>) =>\n    void logLines.push({level, category, message, fields});\n  setLogger({debug: record('debug'), info: record('info'), warn: record('warn'), error: record('error')});\n});\n\nafterEach(async () => {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {\n    const {wss} = hal;\n    await eventually(() => wss.clients.size === 0);\n    await hal.stop();\n  }\n  setLogger();\n});\n\nconst startEngine = async (store: SessionStore, userId: string | number = 'u1') => {\n  const hal = createServer({\n    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    wsAuth: async () => ({id: userId}),\n    sessionStore: store,\n    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n  });\n  await hal.start(0);\n  servers.push(hal);\n  const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n  return `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws`;\n};\n\nconst askAfterConnect = async (url: string, outbound: unknown): Promise<{connected: Frame; frames: Frame[]}> => {\n  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, 't']);\n  clients.push(client);\n  const frames: Frame[] = [];\n  let connected: Frame = {type: ''};\n  await new Promise<void>(resolve => {\nhal-engine-abandon-on-close\nhal-engine-architecture\nhal-engine-awaitable-session-store\nhal-engine-before-tool-call-hook\nhal-engine-chat-routes\nhal-engine-commit-open-entries\nhal-engine-config-hooks\nhal-engine-conversation-list\nhal-engine-doc-gates\nhal-engine-mongo-session-store\nhal-engine-npm-release\nhal-engine-providers\nhal-engine-session-lifetime\nhal-engine-session-resume\nhal-engine-session-write-signal\nhal-engine-single-terminal-frame\nhal-engine-spec-anchor-drift\nhal-engine-spec-link-placement\nhal-engine-spec-status\nhal-engine-subprotocol-marker\nhal-engine-thinking-tag-parser\nhal-engine-tool-budget\nhal-engine-tool-executor-throw\nhal-engine-tool-input-validation\nhal-engine-tool-responses\nhal-engine-websocket-protocol\n# run\n    on every pull request in .github/workflows/ci.yml as the \"Test\" step.\n    Selectors are \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by the\n    list command. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01U43kqL1q3Ed9uEbqRNe84V","type":"tool_result","content":"beforeEach(() => {\n  logLines.length = 0;\n  const record = (level: string) => (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>) =>\n    void logLines.push({level, category, message, fields});\n  setLogger({debug: record('debug'), info: record('info'), warn: record('warn'), error: record('error')});\n});\n\nafterEach(async () => {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {\n    const {wss} = hal;\n    await eventually(() => wss.clients.size === 0);\n    await hal.stop();\n  }\n  setLogger();\n});\n----\n  setLogger({debug: () => undefined, info: () => undefined, warn: () => undefined, error: () => undefined});\n});\n\nafterAll(() => {\n  setLogger();\n});\n\n// Each server sees its sockets close before it stops, so no close is logged after the logger above is restored.\nafterEach(async () => {\n  await closeAll(clients, servers);\n});\n\n// The authenticated user is read per upgrade, so one test can reconnect as someone else.\nconst startEngine = async (\n  store: SessionStore,\n  resume?: SessionResumeOptions,\n  orchestrator: ChatOrchestrator = answeringOrchestrator\n) => {\n----\n---\nadr_number: 6\ntitle: Four re-lint rules suppressed where they misread the domain\nstatus: accepted\ndate: 2026-09-10\ndomains:\n  - tooling\n  - lint\n  - ai-providers\n  - transport\n---\n\n# ADR-006: Four re-lint rules suppressed where they misread the domain\n\nThis ADR records four inline suppressions of `@re-cinq/eslint-plugin-re-lint` rules, and why each is a mismatch between a good rule and this repo's domain rather than debt to be paid down. Every other finding the rules report stays reported: three of the four rules remain enabled and still fire elsewhere in the tree, and the suppressions are per-site with a reason attached, never a rule switched off repo-wide. The alternative in each case was to change working code in a direction that made it worse - a breaking public API change, an eagerly-loaded SDK, a lost type narrowing at a trust boundary, and a deleted regression pin. This decision is implemented.\n\n## Context\n\n`eslint.config.mjs` enables 31 of re-lint's 39 rules, several of which other adopters decline. Enabling them was deliberate: the instruction was to integrate every applicable linter and let it fail rather than refactor to suit it, so the backlog would be visible. Working through that backlog separates cleanly into findings whose fix improves the code and findings whose fix does not. These four are the second kind. They were identified while clearing the mechanical findings, each verified by reading the rule's own source or probing it, not inferred from the message text.\n\n## Decision\n\nFive sites carry `eslint-disable-next-line` (or, in one case, a file-level `eslint-disable`) with a `--` reason referring here, under the four decisions below. Together they account for every finding these three rules report: run with `--no-inline-config` and the count is three `no-flag-params`, one `prefer-polymorphism` and one `test-imports-its-subject`. The sixth was withdrawn when its rule stopped firing - see the `validateMessage` entry.\n\n**`re-lint/no-flag-params` on `AIError`'s `retryable` parameter** (`src/types/ai.ts`). `AIError` is exported from `src/index.ts` and `retryable` is a `public readonly` constructor parameter, so it is part of the published API. The rule's remedy - split the function into two named functions - means two error classes or two factories, which is a breaking change for every consumer constructing an `AIError`. CLAUDE.md routes breaking changes through an issue, a migration guide, a MINOR bump and a deprecation period; a lint sweep is not that process.\n\nThe same constraint reaches every other site that constructs a retryable `AIError`, because `retryable` defaults to `false` and there is no other way to express one. Two carry the same suppression: `mapVertexError` in `src/providers/vertex/vertexProvider.ts`, which classifies a rate limit, and one call site in `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts`, which builds the error a handler is expected to surface. In both the rule's premise is false in the same way - the boolean is not selecting a behaviour, it is a field of a value.\n\nTwo further sites in `src/providers/vertex/vertexProvider.test.ts` carried this suppression until those tests stopped constructing an expected `AIError` at all. Asserting on the thrown error's `name`, `code` and `retryable` fields is both stricter and free of the literal: `expect(...).rejects.toThrow(new AIError(msg, code, true))` compares only the message, so it passed against a deliberately mis-classified error.\n\n**`re-lint/prefer-polymorphism` on `createProvider`** (`src/providers/providerFactory.ts`). The five-way `switch` on `config.type` narrows a discriminated union onto five factories that take five different config types. A lookup table keyed by the string loses that narrowing: TypeScript cannot prove the selected arm's parameter matches the value being dispatched, so the table needs a cast at exactly the point the union was buying safety. This is the same premise as the `validateMessage` entry below, and it is not the premise this entry carried before the ESM conversion. Until then each arm `require`d its provider's module lazily and the suppression was justified as a loading boundary - but `src/index.ts` statically re-exports all five factories, so the factory's laziness never prevented a single module load. The boundary that does work lives inside each provider, around the vendor SDK, and stays there.\n\n**`re-lint/prefer-polymorphism` on `validateMessage`** (`src/transport/ws/validation.ts`). Withdrawn, not reversed. The `switch` on `message.type` narrows an untrusted WebSocket frame through a discriminated union, and a lookup keyed by string would lose that narrowing at precisely the point where the input is attacker-controlled and the type is a claim rather than a fact. The `default` arm rejecting unknown types is the security behaviour. But removing the `send_message` alias left the switch with two cases, below the rule's threshold, so the rule no longer fires and the directive became an unused-disable error. It is deleted. The reasoning above stands and the suppression should come back with it the moment a third case is added - which the add-a-message-type how-to walks a reader straight into.\n\n**`re-lint/test-imports-its-subject` on the layering test** (`scripts/eslint-layers.test.ts`). The rule reports that the file loads no first-party module and so cannot fail when production code changes. It does exercise the real `eslint.config.mjs` - through `spawnSync`, because loading a flat config in-process needs `--experimental-vm-modules` - and the rule cannot see through a subprocess. The file exists because the layering gate's failure mode is silence: `no-cross-layer-import` keys packages by `<package>/src/`, so a misconfigured root matches nothing, reports nothing and looks adopted, which is exactly the state this repo was in until it was probed. Deleting or rewriting the test to satisfy the rule removes the only thing that would notice.\n\n## Rationale\n\nA suppression is honest when the rule's premise is false for the site, and dishonest when it is merely inconvenient. Each of these four fails the rule's premise: the \"function\" is a public constructor, the \"dispatch\" is a loading boundary, the \"dispatch\" is a type narrowing, and the \"test that loads nothing real\" loads the config under test in another process. In each case the rule's suggested remedy is a change that a reviewer would reject on its merits.\n\nPer-site suppression with a written reason was preferred over disabling any rule repo-wide, because three of the four rules have genuine findings elsewhere that should keep failing. `no-flag-params` reports four other sites, `prefer-polymorphism` one, and both counts should fall.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Five findings no longer appear across the four decisions; two of the three rules keep firing elsewhere.\n- A future reader who removes a suppression sees the reason in the `--` clause before deciding.\n- The suppressions are load-bearing in one direction: if `AIError` is ever redesigned through the breaking-change process, or the provider factory becomes async, the corresponding suppression should be reconsidered rather than carried forward.\n- `re-lint/no-forwarding-class` remains enabled and inert for an unrelated reason - it needs type information this config does not provide - and is documented at its site in `eslint.config.mjs`, not here.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n**Refactor to satisfy each rule.** Rejected: a breaking API change outside the documented process, an eager-loading regression, a lost narrowing at a trust boundary, and a deleted anti-silence pin.\n\n**Disable the three rules repo-wide.** Rejected: the suppressions are per-site and visible, and each names its reason. Turning the rules off would silence them for code not yet written, which is where their value is.\n\n**Leave all four failing.** Rejected: a gate that reports findings nobody intends to act on trains readers to ignore it, and these four would never be acted on.\n\n## References\n\n- `eslint.config.mjs` — the enabled rule set and the `no-forwarding-class` inertness note\n----\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must have a type field'};\n  }\n\n  switch (message.type) {\n    case 'user_message':\n      return validateUserMessage(message);\n    case 'ping':\n      return validatePingMessage(message);\n----\n43:    \"test\": \"node --experimental-vm-modules node_modules/jest/bin/jest.js\",\n50:    \"check:spec-links\": \"node scripts/check-spec-links.mjs\",\n51: {\n----\n---\nadr_number: 6\ntitle: Four re-lint rules suppressed where they misread the domain\nstatus: accepted\ndate: 2026-09-10\ndomains:\n  - tooling\n  - lint\n  - ai-providers\n  - transport\n---\n\n# ADR-006: Four re-lint rules suppressed where they misread the domain\n\nThis ADR records four inline suppressions of `@re-cinq/eslint-plugin-re-lint` rules, and why each is a mismatch between a good rule and this repo's domain rather than debt to be paid down. Every other finding the rules report stays reported: three of the four rules remain enabled and still fire elsewhere in the tree, and the suppressions are per-site with a reason attached, never a rule switched off repo-wide. The alternative in each case was to change working code in a direction that made it worse - a breaking public API change, an eagerly-loaded SDK, a lost type narrowing at a trust boundary, and a deleted regression pin. This decision is implemented.\n\n## Context\n\n`eslint.config.mjs` enables 31 of re-lint's 39 rules, several of which other adopters decline. Enabling them was deliberate: the instruction was to integrate every applicable linter and let it fail rather than refactor to suit it, so the backlog would be visible. Working through that backlog separates cleanly into findings whose fix improves the code and findings whose fix does not. These four are the second kind. They were identified while clearing the mechanical findings, each verified by reading the rule's own source or probing it, not inferred from the message text.\n\n## Decision\n\nFive sites carry `eslint-disable-next-line` (or, in one case, a file-level `eslint-disable`) with a `--` reason referring here, under the four decisions below. Together they account for every finding these three rules report: run with `--no-inline-config` and the count is three `no-flag-params`, one `prefer-polymorphism` and one `test-imports-its-subject`. The sixth was withdrawn when its rule stopped firing - see the `validateMessage` entry.\n\n**`re-lint/no-flag-params` on `AIError`'s `retryable` parameter** (`src/types/ai.ts`). `AIError` is exported from `src/index.ts` and `retryable` is a `public readonly` constructor parameter, so it is part of the published API. The rule's remedy - split the function into two named functions - means two error classes or two factories, which is a breaking change for every consumer constructing an `AIError`. CLAUDE.md routes breaking changes through an issue, a migration guide, a MINOR bump and a deprecation period; a lint sweep is not that process.\n\nThe same constraint reaches every other site that constructs a retryable `AIError`, because `retryable` defaults to `false` and there is no other way to express one. Two carry the same suppression: `mapVertexError` in `src/providers/vertex/vertexProvider.ts`, which classifies a rate limit, and one call site in `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.test.ts`, which builds the error a handler is expected to surface. In both the rule's premise is false in the same way - the boolean is not selecting a behaviour, it is a field of a value.\n\nTwo further sites in `src/providers/vertex/vertexProvider.test.ts` carried this suppression until those tests stopped constructing an expected `AIError` at all. Asserting on the thrown error's `name`, `code` and `retryable` fields is both stricter and free of the literal: `expect(...).rejects.toThrow(new AIError(msg, code, true))` compares only the message, so it passed against a deliberately mis-classified error.\n\n**`re-lint/prefer-polymorphism` on `createProvider`** (`src/providers/providerFactory.ts`). The five-way `switch` on `config.type` narrows a discriminated union onto five factories that take five different config types. A lookup table keyed by the string loses that narrowing: TypeScript cannot prove the selected arm's parameter matches the value being dispatched, so the table needs a cast at exactly the point the union was buying safety. This is the same premise as the `validateMessage` entry below, and it is not the premise this entry carried before the ESM conversion. Until then each arm `require`d its provider's module lazily and the suppression was justified as a loading boundary - but `src/index.ts` statically re-exports all five factories, so the factory's laziness never prevented a single module load. The boundary that does work lives inside each provider, around the vendor SDK, and stays there.\n\n**`re-lint/prefer-polymorphism` on `validateMessage`** (`src/transport/ws/validation.ts`). Withdrawn, not reversed. The `switch` on `message.type` narrows an untrusted WebSocket frame through a discriminated union, and a lookup keyed by string would lose that narrowing at precisely the point where the input is attacker-controlled and the type is a claim rather than a fact. The `default` arm rejecting unknown types is the security behaviour. But removing the `send_message` alias left the switch with two cases, below the rule's threshold, so the rule no longer fires and the directive became an unused-disable error. It is deleted. The reasoning above stands and the suppression should come back with it the moment a third case is added - which the add-a-message-type how-to walks a reader straight into.\n\n**`re-lint/test-imports-its-subject` on the layering test** (`scripts/eslint-layers.test.ts`). The rule reports that the file loads no first-party module and so cannot fail when production code changes. It does exercise the real `eslint.config.mjs` - through `spawnSync`, because loading a flat config in-process needs `--experimental-vm-modules` - and the rule cannot see through a subprocess. The file exists because the layering gate's failure mode is silence: `no-cross-layer-import` keys packages by `<package>/src/`, so a misconfigured root matches nothing, reports nothing and looks adopted, which is exactly the state this repo was in until it was probed. Deleting or rewriting the test to satisfy the rule removes the only thing that would notice.\n\n## Rationale\n\nA suppression is honest when the rule's premise is false for the site, and dishonest when it is merely inconvenient. Each of these four fails the rule's premise: the \"function\" is a public constructor, the \"dispatch\" is a loading boundary, the \"dispatch\" is a type narrowing, and the \"test that loads nothing real\" loads the config under test in another process. In each case the rule's suggested remedy is a change that a reviewer would reject on its merits.\n\nPer-site suppression with a written reason was preferred over disabling any rule repo-wide, because three of the four rules have genuine findings elsewhere that should keep failing. `no-flag-params` reports four other sites, `prefer-polymorphism` one, and both counts should fall.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Five findings no longer appear across the four decisions; two of the three rules keep firing elsewhere.\n- A future reader who removes a suppression sees the reason in the `--` clause before deciding.\n- The suppressions are load-bearing in one direction: if `AIError` is ever redesigned through the breaking-change process, or the provider factory becomes async, the corresponding suppression should be reconsidered rather than carried forward.\n- `re-lint/no-forwarding-class` remains enabled and inert for an unrelated reason - it needs type information this config does not provide - and is documented at its site in `eslint.config.mjs`, not here.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n**Refactor to satisfy each rule.** Rejected: a breaking API change outside the documented process, an eager-loading regression, a lost narrowing at a trust boundary, and a deleted anti-silence pin.\n\n**Disable the three rules repo-wide.** Rejected: the suppressions are per-site and visible, and each names its reason. Turning the rules off would silence them for code not yet written, which is where their value is.\n\n**Leave all four failing.** Rejected: a gate that reports findings nobody intends to act on trains readers to ignore it, and these four would never be acted on.\n\n## References\n\n- `eslint.config.mjs` — the enabled rule set and the `no-forwarding-class` inertness note\n----\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must have a type field'};\n  }\n\n  switch (message.type) {\n    case 'user_message':\n      return validateUserMessage(message);\n    case 'ping':\n      return validatePingMessage(message);\n----\n43:    \"test\": \"node --experimental-vm-modules node_modules/jest/bin/jest.js\",\n50:    \"check:spec-links\": \"node scripts/check-spec-links.mjs\",\n51: --fix\",\n62:    \"verify\": \"npm run typecheck && npm run typecheck:example && npm run eslint && npm run lint:backlog && npm run prettier:check && npm run test:coverage && npm run build && npm run userId](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L80)).\nspecs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md:75:- A store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}`, and a throwing `listFor` logs an error on the `ws` category and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}` ([validated by: answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L121)).\nspecs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md:76:- The validator accepts an optional non-negative integer `limit` and an optional `before` cursor whose `sessionId` is 1–128 characters and whose `updatedAt` parses as a valid timestamp, rejecting any other `list_sessions` payload with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without calling `listFor` ([validated by: accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L150)).\nspecs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md:71:- An upgrade whose `Host` header is missing or empty (RFC 6455 §4.1 requires one), or whose request-target is not a URL, such as `//`, is answered with HTTP 400 ([validated by: answers an empty Host, a missing Host and a // target with 400 instead of throwing out of the upgrade listener](../../src/transport/malformedUpgrade.test.ts#L68)).\nspecs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md:72:- The path check reads the target alone and never throws: a path beginning with `{basePath}/ws` passes, as §2.1 says; any other path, an empty target or `//` fails ([validated by: accepts any path beginning with /hal/ws and refuses another path, an empty target and //, without throwing](../../src/transport/ws/wsPath.test.ts#L4)).","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01U43kqL1q3Ed9uEbqRNe84V","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

changed, 93 insertions(+), 38 deletions(-)\ndiff --git a/src/transport/ws/validation.ts b/src/transport/ws/validation.ts\nindex 242d2dd..8026551 100644\n--- a/src/transport/ws/validation.ts\n+++ b/src/transport/ws/validation.ts\n@@ -1,6 +1,7 @@\n import type {IncomingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\n \n const MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH = 10000;\n+const MAX_SESSION_ID_LENGTH = 128;\n \n field'};\n   }\n \n+  // eslint-disable-next-line re-lint/prefer-polymorphism -- a discriminated union narrows the untrusted frame; see adrs/ADR-006-lint-suppressions.md\n   switch (message.type) {\n     case 'user_message':\n       return validateUserMessage(message);\n@@ -64,17 +66,33 @@ function validateUserMessage(message: Record<string, unknown>): ValidationResult\n function validateListSessionsMessage(message: Record<string, unknown>): ValidationResult {\n   const {limit, before} = message;\n \n-  if (limit !== undefined && (typeof limit !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(limit) || limit < 0)) {\n+  if (limit !== undefined && !isNonNegativeInteger(limit)) {\n     return {valid: false, error: 'list_sessions limit must be a non-negative integer'};\n   }\n \n-  if (before !== undefined && (typeof before !== 'object' || before === null)) {\n+  if (before !== undefined && !isCursorObject(before)) {\n     return {valid: false, error: 'list_sessions Number.isInteger(value) && value >= 0;\n+}\n+\n+function isCursorObject(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {\n+  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null;\n+}\n+\n+function isValidSessionId(sessionId: unknown): boolean {\n+  return typeof sessionId === 'string' && sessionId.length >= 1 && sessionId.length <= MAX_SESSION_ID_LENGTH;\n+}\n+\n function
